[293:40] We are looking at the 12 a.m. Eastern Standard Time opening price. Okay, this is very important. When we say we're looking at the midnight price, we're talking about the midnight open in Eastern Standard Time. So, for example, if you're in California
[294:00] or you're somewhere in Europe or, you know, on the West Coast, you are not going to be using the 12 a.m. opening price of your time zone. [294:20] open Eastern Standard Time opening price is right here 25,12 right so the way the strategy works is it relies on the fact that there is data that around 70% of the time the market in the New York session will retrace to the midnight open in the
[294:40] morning okay so if we are below the midnight open you have a roughly 70% chance that the market will come back up to touch that midnight open level. This is after 9:30. Okay, after 9:30 open. Now, same thing to the down like in the opposite direction. If the market is
[295:00] opening above the midnight open, let's say in this case, the market was actually opening above the midnight open at 9:30, there would be a 70% chance that the market will trade down to touch the midnight open. That's why it's called the midnight retracement setup. Now the way I trade it is I have a very clear
[295:20] entry signal which is the first inversion fair value gap that presents after 900 a.m. Okay. So in 4 minutes we might have a trade if this inversion setup plays out. So this
[295:40] is a fiveminute fair value gap. You see the market tapped into it went lower. Fine. No trade yet.
